filter: none; opacity: 1;"><span class="RCsuha_index-number">[<!-- -->5<!-- -->]</span><span class="RCsuha_index-leader" aria-hidden="true">................................................................................................................................................................</span><span>Claude Mythos 5.1</span></span></a></nav></div><span class="RCsuha_credit">Made with Fable 5.1</span><div class="RCsuha_looks" role="group" aria-label="Time of day"><button type="button" class="RCsuha_look RCsuha_on" title="Noon" aria-label="Noon" style="--sw: #7ea9de;">Noon</button><button type="button" class="RCsuha_look" title="Night" aria-label="Night" style="--sw: #1a2237;">Night</button><button type="button" class="RCsuha_look" title="Morning" aria-label="Morning" style="--sw: #dcc4b3;">Morning</button></div></header><article class="_6Ouj0G_article"><div class="PA4F1q_body JsRY2q_launch-body" data-article-body="true" data-theme="ivory"><span id="introduction" class="JsRY2q_anchor"></span><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column PA4F1q_article-summary">We’re introducing Claude Fable 5.1 and Claude Mythos 5.1. They’re the world’s most advanced models for coding and knowledge work—and their research capabilities offer an early glimpse of how AI models will contribute to scientific progress.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Fable 5.1 and Claude Mythos 5.1 are the same model, but with different levels of safeguards. Fable 5.1 is generally available, while Mythos 5.1 is available only through our trusted access programs; its safeguards are specifically designed to support work in cybersecurity and the life sciences.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Alongside its increased capabilities, Fable 5.1 takes important steps towards addressing the feedback we’ve received from customers on price, data retention, and safeguards.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Price<em>.</em></strong> Fable 5.1 will cost an estimated 25% less than Fable 5 for typical workloads, wherever usage is billed by token. This is because we’re reducing our pricing on cache reads (where the model reads inputs that have already been processed and stored). For highly agentic work, the savings will often be much larger—up to approximately 45%.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Data retention<em>. </em></strong>Our new system of Enterprise Frontier Safeguards (EFS) gives customers complete privacy (the same as a zero data retention policy) while still being state-of-the-art at preventing adversarial use. EFS works by storing data in cloud infrastructure controlled entirely by the customer, not Anthropic. It will be made available to enterprise customers in phases, beginning later this fall. Until EFS is available, eligible customers will be able to use Fable 5.1 with zero data retention.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Safeguards<em>. </em></strong>We’ve improved our safeguards to reduce false positives (where the system flags benign content). In cybersecurity, our newest safeguards block 60% fewer false positives than before. In part, this is because Fable 5.1 can now be used to discover software vulnerabilities—though not to develop exploits for them. In biology, we’ve established an access program, developed in partnership with the US government, to enable access to Claude Mythos 5.1’s advanced biology capabilities. We expect to open enrollment for scientists soon.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="frontier">A new performance frontier</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Fable 5.1 sets a new standard for coding, knowledge work, and long-running problem-solving tasks. The charts below show that Fable 5.1 is capable of much higher performance than its predecessor, Fable 5. And when set to Low or Medium effort, Fable 5.1 achieves results similar to or better than Fable 5’s at a much lower cost. (Note that Fable 5.1 defaults to High effort in Claude Code, and to Medium in Claude Cowork and on Claude.ai.)</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="viewSwitcher"><section JQQKta_rich"><p>CursorBench 3.2.0 by cost (log scale), at each effort level.</p></div></figure></div></div></div></section></div><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Fable 5.1 avoids shortcuts that result in poorer-quality work, and it’s smart enough to fix the root causes of software issues. For example, in testing by the investment firm Millennium, Fable 5.1 found the cause of a rare crash in its internal systems that none of its engineers (or any other model) had been able to explain after several years of trying.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Here, you can see how Fable 5.1 compares across various benchmarks:</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" class="zKqBAG_caption JQQKta_rich"><p>Fable 5.1 was evaluated with its production safeguards enabled. On tasks where these safeguards intervened, Fable 5.1 and Fable 5 scored a zero on OSWorld 2.0, and Fable 5 scored a zero on AutomationBench. In all other interventions from our safeguards, cybersecurity tasks were completed by Claude Opus 4.8, and biology tasks were completed by Claude Opus 5. This likely reduces the performance of Fable 5.1 and Fable 5 on these benchmarks.</p></figcaption></figure></div><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Our early-access partners noticed these performance upgrades, and also picked up on more qualitative improvements in the model’s outputs. aria-labelledby="_R_g7inpfkq6btb_-tab-0"><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 What we found—which includes the early examples we share below—adds to the evidence that AI models will soon make important contributions to scientific discovery.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Molecular design<em>.</em> </strong>Many modern medicines work by binding to targets within the body to block, activate, or deliver something to them. High-affinity binders are necessary for drugs to work at lower doses; designing one is the first step in the development process for many common drug modalities. To see how well Claude Mythos 5.1 could do at this task, we gave the model access to open-source protein design and folding tools and sent its designs to two external organizations for experimental validation. Mythos 5.1 proved able to design very high-affinity binders. On three targets, <sup class="caption PA4F1q_sup">[3]</sup> its binding affinities were 10 times higher than the best designs submitted to <a id="base-ui-_R_7qk7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://proteinbase.com/competitions" rel="noopener noreferrer">Adaptyv Bio’s protein design competitions</a>. Its hit rate (that is, the number of designs that were viable binders) was the strongest we’ve measured to date: it reached nearly 50% across 12 targets. (Hit rates of 10–15% are typical in protein design today.)</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="videoSwitcher"><figure class="xFM4Hq_video-switcher"><div class="xFM4Hq_frame"><video class="xFM4Hq_poster" src="https://origin-ten-jet.vercel.app/sites/anthropic/pages-assets/7b7206e6f99097ec105a.webm" poster="https://origin-ten-jet.vercel.app/sites/anthropic/pages-assets/7086876e41affdb69862.jpg" loop="" muted="" playsinline=""></video><button type="button" class="xFM4Hq_play" aria-label="Play: Claude-designed protein binders (orange) for each of 12 targets (grey). Every design in the video was confirmed to bind in the lab. Structures shown are ESMFold2 predictions."><svg viewBox="0 0 24 24" width="24" height="24" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M7 4 L20 12 L7 20 Z"></path></svg></button></div><figcaption class="xFM4Hq_caption">Claude-designed protein binders (orange) for each of 12 targets (grey). Every design in the video was confirmed to bind in the lab. Structures shown are ESMFold2 predictions.</figcaption></figure></div><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Computational analysis and modeling</strong>. Claude Fable 5.1 trained a neural network to create a new, high-resolution elevation map of a third of the planet Venus. Its work was based on radar images taken by NASA’s Magellan mission more than 30 years ago and a <a id="base-ui-_R_7mm7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://agupubs.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1029/2012EO120002" rel="noopener noreferrer">map</a> that already existed for one-fifth of the planet. Claude’s new map now reveals details down to two to three kilometers, rather than 10 to 20, and shows heights up to 25% more accurately than before.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">We’re <a id="base-ui-_R_3sn7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://zenodo.org/records/22164484" rel="noopener noreferrer">releasing this map</a> under a Creative Commons license in advance of upcoming NASA VERITAS and ESA EnVision missions, in hopes that it might help them determine which geologic features to target for future observation.</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="mediaGallery" data-width="full-page"><div class="_6QZEyW_media-gallery-wrapper"><div class="_6QZEyW_media-gallery-container"><div class="_6QZEyW_media-gallery"><figure class="_6QZEyW_media-item"><div class="_6QZEyW_media-content"><img alt="Radar image (Magellan): bright cone, radian lava flows" loading="lazy" width="800" class="_6QZEyW_media-item"><div class="_6QZEyW_media-content"><img alt="Altimetry 10-20km footprint" loading="lazy" width="800" height="800" decoding="async" data-nimg="1" class="_6QZEyW_media-image" footprint</figcaption></figure><figure class="_6QZEyW_media-item"><div class="_6QZEyW_media-content"><img alt="New DEM (300m) a volcano 15km across" loading="lazy" width="800" height="800" decoding="async" data-nimg="1" class="_6QZEyW_media-image" style="color:transparent" src="https://origin-ten-jet.vercel.app/sites/anthropic/pages-assets/7007a15acf7ec0599ff9.webp"></div><figcaption class="caption">New DEM (300m) a volcano 15km across</figcaption></figure><figure class="_6QZEyW_media-item"><div class="_6QZEyW_media-content"><div class="_6QZEyW_media-video"><video autoplay="" controls="" playsinline="" muted="" loop="" src="https://origin-ten-jet.vercel.app/sites/anthropic/pages-assets/c6cb218264f00b8853af.mp4"></video></div></div><figcaption class="caption">A small shield volcano on Venus</figcaption></figure></div></div></div></div><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Computational biology<em>.</em></strong> In computational biology, it’s common to run task-specific machine learning models on GPUs. The speed of these models is therefore a bottleneck to research progress. Mythos 5.1 provided one solution to this problem: by writing custom GPU kernels and caching their intermediate results, it sped up seven open-source deep learning models by up to 2.5 times (with identical outputs).</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">The benefits of such speed-ups accumulate quickly. In any given experiment, biologists might run these models thousands of times (for example, testing every possible mutation near every human gene). On analyses like these, the optimized models cut estimated GPU costs by 30–60%. This kind of optimization would normally take a team of performance engineers weeks, and is often unaffordable for academic labs. Mythos 5.1 was able to do it in just days, using the publicly available source code alone. We plan to open-source these optimizations soon.</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="viewSwitcher"><section class="kN30kq_root"><div class="kN30kq_tabs" role="tablist" Evo 2 40B saves more on a whole job (2.3x) than per forward (1.4x) because some of its optimizations only pay off across many sequences.</p></div></figure></div></div></div></section></div><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As our models’ scientific capabilities improve, our investment in scientific progress is also growing. Last week, we previewed the <a target="_blank" href="/news/model-hardware-standard-research-preview" rel="noopener noreferrer">Model Hardware Standard</a>, which allows Claude to directly and safely operate laboratory equipment. We’ve also recently <a target="_blank" href="/news/expanding-support-for-scientists" rel="noopener noreferrer">expanded our support for scientists</a> through our <a target="_blank" href="/news/ai-for-science-program" rel="noopener noreferrer">AI for Science program</a>, which provides free credits to researchers working on high-impact scientific projects, and we are offering steeply discounted usage through our new <a id="base-ui-_R_fgs7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://claude.com/programs/team-plan-for-scientists" rel="noopener noreferrer">Claude Team plan for scientists</a>.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="safety-security-and-alignment">Safety, security, and alignment</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">AI models’ agentic capabilities have become much more powerful over the past two years. But as we’ve <a target="_blank" href="/news/investigating-incidents-cybersecurity-evals" rel="noopener noreferrer">documented</a>, greater autonomy comes with new risks. Work on safety, security, and alignment needs to advance at the same pace as AI capabilities. Yesterday, we <a target="_blank" href="/news/improving-alignment-security-efforts" rel="noopener noreferrer">published a report</a> describing how we are improving our own alignment and security efforts</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Prior to releasing Claude Fable 5.1 and Claude Mythos 5.1, we (and, in some cases, external researchers) subjected the models to extensive testing for risks across many areas. We describe these efforts in full in our <a target="_blank" href="/claude-fable-5-1-mythos-5-1-system-card" rel="noopener noreferrer">System Card</a>; below is a brief summary.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Chemical and biological risks</strong>. We tested the extent to which Claude Mythos 5.1 could help create chemical or biological weapons. This involved expert red-teaming, automated evaluations, and a tabletop exercise that paired PhD-level biologists with AI experts, testing whether the models could match human specialists’ performance. Mythos 5.1’s capabilities are greater than those of Mythos 5. However, our evaluations indicate that it still falls short of the next risk tier defined in our <a target="_blank" href="/responsible-scaling-policy" rel="noopener noreferrer">Responsible Scaling Policy</a>. We are therefore deploying Mythos 5.1 with the <a target="_blank" href="/news/improving-fable-5-s-biology-safeguards" rel="noopener noreferrer">same safeguards</a> that we applied to Mythos 5, which restrict access to research biology capabilities.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Cyber risks</strong>. We ran a suite of evaluations to assess the cyber capabilities of Claude Mythos 5.1 (with cybersecurity safeguards off). Overall, the model demonstrates the strongest cyber capabilities of any model we’ve released, though it still falls within the lower category of risk in our <a target="_blank" href="/news/compliance-framework-SB53" rel="noopener noreferrer">Frontier Compliance Framework</a>. We also performed extensive stress-testing of our cybersecurity safeguards for Fable 5.1: in addition to our own dynamic evaluation of their robustness, we commissioned external testing from two organizations, along with automated testing by <a id="base-ui-_R_fb27inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://www.grayswan.ai/" rel="noopener noreferrer">Gray Swan</a>. As with Fable 5 and Opus 5, we have not found evidence of a <a target="_blank" href="/news/fable-safeguards-jailbreak-framework" rel="noopener noreferrer">critical-severity jailbreak</a> for these safeguards.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Agentic safety</strong>. We ran evaluations of how Claude Mythos 5.1 responds to malicious requests and prompt injections (adversarial instructions hidden within content processed by AI models). It refused malicious agentic coding and computer use requests at a comparable rate to Mythos 5, Sonnet 5, and Opus 5, and it is our most robust model to date on an external <a target="_blank" href="/claude-fable-5-1-mythos-5-1-system-card" rel="noopener noreferrer">prompt injection benchmark</a>.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Alignment<em>.</em></strong> We tested the model’s behavior through static and interactive behavioral evaluations, analyses of its internal thinking using <a target="_blank" href="/research/natural-language-autoencoders" rel="noopener noreferrer">natural language autoencoders</a>, misalignment-related capability evaluations, a review of our training data, and analyses of our internal pilot use. We also received reports from external testing.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Our automated behavioral audit found that Claude Mythos 5.1 is better aligned across most metrics than its predecessor, Mythos 5. The model is significantly less likely than Mythos 5 to try to access resources outside of its test environment when assigned an otherwise impossible task. It is also less likely than Mythos 5 to use motivated reasoning to justify its actions (for instance, by reasoning that the situation is a simulation or evaluation), and it is less likely to ignore explicit constraints in pursuit of users’ goals. From our review of its training data, Mythos 5.1 both attempts reward hacking (or cheating), and succeeds at it, at a lower overall rate than Mythos 5.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Though generally our alignment evaluations showed improvements, our testing found the model can still sometimes bypass approvals and auto-mode classifiers (as we discuss in more detail in our <a target="_blank" href="/claude-fable-5-1-mythos-5-1-system-card" rel="noopener noreferrer">System Card</a>). There are also limitations to the coverage provided by our alignment assessment. Currently, our automated behavioral audit provides less visibility into very long-context work and multi-agent settings. We also have less coverage of impossible tasks (which can elicit more abnormal and misaligned behavior) than we’d like, although we’ve recently made improvements in this domain and are working hard to continue doing so.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">We have also improved our safeguards so that they allow our models to be more useful without compromising on safety. We describe these changes below.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Automated safeguards for enterprises<em>.</em></strong> <a target="_blank" href="/news/enterprise-frontier-safeguards" rel="noopener noreferrer">Enterprise Frontier Safeguards</a> (EFS) allows us to detect and respond to misuse of our models while still providing our enterprise customers the privacy of a zero data retention agreement. With EFS, customers store their data on their own cloud infrastructure, rather than on Anthropic’s systems; any human review is, by default, done by the customer themselves, rather than Anthropic. We developed EFS in close collaboration with more than 100 customers across industries like financial services, healthcare, manufacturing, telecom, law, retail, and the public sector, and with our cloud partners at Amazon Web Services, Google Cloud, and Microsoft Azure.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">EFS will be supported on Claude Code, Claude Enterprise, the Claude Platform, Amazon Bedrock, Claude Platform on AWS, Google’s Agent Platform, and Microsoft Foundry. It’s rolling out in phases, starting this fall. As noted above, customers who are eligible for EFS can use Fable 5.1 (and Fable 5) with zero data retention until EFS is ready. You can read more about EFS <a target="_blank" href="/news/enterprise-frontier-safeguards" rel="noopener noreferrer">here</a>; to request access, please complete <a id="base-ui-_R_7p97inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" href="https://claude.com/form/enterprise-frontier-safeguards">this form</a>.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>More precise safeguards for biology and cybersecurity<em>. </em></strong>In the past few months, we’ve made progress in making our safeguards for Fable 5.1 more precise: ensuring that they’re less likely to flag benign content (like queries about medical issues or cyberdefenders using the model to make their systems safer), but still ensuring they provide robust protection against genuine threats.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As we <a target="_blank" href="/news/improving-fable-5-s-biology-safeguards" rel="noopener noreferrer">recently shared</a>, our latest biology safeguards for Fable 5.1 and Fable 5 fire 85% less often for benign requests related to elementary biology and medical questions (relative to those that launched with Fable 5). However, queries related to research and development in the life sciences will still be directed to our Opus models. We’re making the model’s life sciences capabilities available to professionals through an access program for Claude Mythos 5.1 that we’ve developed in partnership with the US government, which we discuss below.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">With Fable 5.1, we’re updating our cybersecurity safeguards to be more precise. We’re also now allowing Fable 5.1 to be used for identifying software vulnerabilities—that is, to conduct the kind of defensive work that improves software security. As a result of these changes, Claude Code users can expect an average of around 60% fewer interventions per session from our cyber safeguards, relative to the previous safeguards on Fable 5. Our safeguards do, however, still redirect several kinds of dual-use cybersecurity tasks (tasks that might have helpful <em>or</em> harmful applications) to our Opus models. This includes penetration testing, exploit generation, and binary-based vulnerability scanning.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Anti-distillation mechanisms<em>.</em></strong> Distillation is a method used to extract the capabilities of advanced models. It is often employed on an industrial scale, using thousands of fake accounts. Distillation is a safety risk, since the distilled capabilities can subsequently be released without adequate safeguards. Fable 5.1 comes with strengthened mechanisms to make distillation attacks harder. For example, it is no longer possible for new API accounts (those created from today onwards) to manually edit Claude’s prior context in a multi-turn conversation while preserving the transcript of Claude’s prior thinking. This closes off a common, publicly documented distillation technique, which allowed distillers to illicitly extract Claude’s thinking. We’re rolling out the change gradually, to minimize disruption: existing accounts are not currently affected by this change, though it will apply to all users with future model releases. A small number of customers’ custom integrations will then be affected. Our <a id="base-ui-_R_7nd7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/16761192" rel="noopener noreferrer">Help Center article</a> explains more about this change and the adjustments that developers can make.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="mythos">Trusted access for Claude Mythos 5.1</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Mythos 5.1 is identical to Fable 5.1, but it offers more permissive safeguards for vetted individuals and organizations whose work is affected by the cybersecurity and life sciences restrictions outlined above. It will be available through two trusted access programs:</p><ul class="PA4F1q_reading-column text-body-1-serif post-text"><li><strong>Cyber Verification Program:</strong> The CVP currently provides access to certain Opus- and Sonnet-class models with reduced cyber safeguards for defensive security work. In the near future, this program will also include access to Claude Mythos-class models. <a id="base-ui-_R_urh7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://portal.anthropic.com/programs/cvp" rel="noopener noreferrer">Apply to join the CVP here</a>.</li><li><strong>Life Sciences Verification Program:</strong> The LSVP is designed so that life sciences professionals can use Claude Mythos 5.1 with safeguards designed for professional research and development activities (while all other safeguards remain in place). In partnership with the US government, we have enrolled our first participants, and we plan to expand access to this program to the broader life sciences community.</li></ul><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">In addition to these trusted access programs, <a id="base-ui-_R_3ti7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://claude.com/product/claude-security" rel="noopener noreferrer">Claude Security</a>, our product that scans codebases for vulnerabilities and suggests patches for human review, is now also powered by Claude Mythos 5.1.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="compliance-with-the-eu-ai-act">Compliance with the EU AI Act</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">In July 2026, Anthropic (along with <a id="base-ui-_R_3tk7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/news/strong-backing-code-practice-transparency-ai-generated-content" rel="noopener noreferrer">190 other signatories</a>, including several other major AI model providers) signed the EU AI Act’s Code of Practice on Transparency of AI-Generated Content.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">This required us to add a watermark—a numerical way of determining the likelihood that Claude was involved in writing a piece of text—to the outputs of models released after August 2, 2026. As we <a target="_blank" href="/news/claude-text-watermark" rel="noopener noreferrer">recently explained</a>, this watermark is invisible to anyone who does not have the detection API. It has no practical impact on the quality or content of Claude’s outputs and contains no information about the user, their organization, or their conversations with Claude.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">The Act also required us to provide a way for users to tell whether a text likely contains the watermark. We are thus rolling out a detection API in private preview. It is currently available to eligible organizations (such as regulators, law enforcement, media, fact-checkers, independent researchers, educational organizations, and EU civil society groups) as required under EU law. It is also available for enterprises that are similarly obligated to verify watermarking for their own compliance with the Act. We plan to expand access to the detection API over time. You can register interest in access <a id="base-ui-_R_3tm7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://forms.gle/9tGA33hPJJwtHsMk9" rel="noopener noreferrer">here</a>.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="cost-and-availability">Cost and availability</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Fable 5.1 is available today on all platforms, including Amazon Web Services, Google Cloud, and Microsoft Azure. Developers can get started with <code class="nsPAba_code">claude-fable-5-1</code> on the Claude API.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As mentioned above, we have reduced the price of Fable 5.1’s cache reads (where the model reuses context it has already processed) wherever usage is billed by token, such as on our API. Cache reads now cost 75% less, or $0.25 per million tokens.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">This change leads to a substantial reduction in the overall cost of running the model. For typical workloads, costs are reduced by around 25% relative to Fable 5. For complex coding and highly agentic tasks, the savings could be up to around 45%. The graph below illustrates why this change makes such a big difference:</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="chart"><figure class="_3ia3wq_frame"><div class="_3ia3wq_panel"><figcaption In parallel, we’re continuing our work to bring many of the improvements of Fable 5.1 to the rest of our model family.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As discussed above, Claude Mythos 5.1 is available to vetted cyberdefenders and life scientists. Currently, it is only available to a set of US organizations, though we’re coordinating with the US government to expand access to a broader set of domestic and international partners as quickly as possible. To register interest in access to Claude Mythos 5.1 for cyberdefense through the CVP, <a id="base-ui-_R_3tu7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://portal.anthropic.com/programs/cvp" rel="noopener noreferrer">head here</a>.</p></div><section id="get-started" class="qvEXQG_get-started _6Ouj0G_reading-column"><h2 class="text-body-1-serif qvEXQG_heading">Get started</h2><div class="qvEXQG_actions"><a href="https://claude.ai/" class="f9ZZrG_button f9ZZrG_corner-small f9ZZrG_variant-primary f9ZZrG_size-medium" rel="noopener" target="_blank">Try Claude</a><a href="https://platform.claude.com/" class="f9ZZrG_button f9ZZrG_corner-small f9ZZrG_variant-secondary f9ZZrG_size-medium" rel="noopener" target="_blank">Start building</a></div></section><section id="footnotes" class="_FS4_a_footnotes _6Ouj0G_reading-column"><h2 class="text-body-1-serif _FS4_a_heading">Footnotes</h2><div class="_FS4_a_columns"><p class="_FS4_a_note"><sup class="_FS4_a_label">1</sup> <strong>Terminal-Bench-Science 0.1:</strong> The standard error is ±3.5–4.5 pts per model. The public leaderboard (3 trials/task, Claude Code harness) reports Claude Opus 5 at 30.0% and Claude Fable 5 at 21.4%; our setup reproduces them at 29.0% and 24.7%, respectively, both within noise.</p><p class="_FS4_a_note"><sup class="_FS4_a_label">2</sup> <strong>OSWorld 2.0:</strong> Scores are on the benchmark authors’ August 2026 task release; Fable 5 and Opus 5 were re-run under the same conditions. Because the task files differ from earlier releases, these numbers aren't directly comparable to previously published OSWorld 2.0 results, which is why no competitor score is shown</p><p class="_FS4_a_note"><sup class="_FS4_a_label">3</sup> <!-- -->These three targets are (EGFR, Nipah G, 15-PGDH) and come from <a href="https://proteinbase.com/competitions" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Adaptyv Bio’s protein design competitions</a>. The Nipah G comparison is against <em>de novo</em> designs targeting the receptor-binding site on the G head (best: ~8–12 nM, <a href="https://proteinbase.com/proteins/shy-otter-jade" target="_blank" rel="noopener">N1032</a>). A <em>de novo</em> entry from <a href="https://blog.escalante.bio/winning-the-de-novo-portion-of-the-adaptyv-nipah-binder-competition/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Nick Boyd/Escalante Bio</a> that targets a different region (the stalk) reached ~1.4 nM (<a href="https://proteinbase.com/proteins/shy-eagle-fern" target="_blank" rel="noopener">design_7</a>), comparable to our best binder.</p></div></section><section class="Y_7aRW_end-matter _6Ouj0G_reading-column"><div id="further-reading" class="Y_7aRW_column"><h2 class="text-body-1-serif Y_7aRW_heading">Further 11:T25686,<span data-nav-hide-on-scroll="true" hidden=""></span><div class="_6Ouj0G_page theme-ivory"><nav class="_3hrKvq_side-toc" aria-label="Contents" style="top: 422px;"><ol class="_3hrKvq_list"><li><a href="#introduction" class="_3hrKvq_link _3hrKvq_is-active" draggable="false"><span class="_3hrKvq_pill">Introduction</span></a></li><li><a href="#frontier" class="_3hrKvq_link" draggable="false"><span class="_3hrKvq_pill">A new performance frontier</span></a></li><li><a href="#scientific-research" class="_3hrKvq_link" draggable="false"><span class="_3hrKvq_pill">Scientific research</span></a></li><li><a href="#safety-security-and-alignment" class="_3hrKvq_link" draggable="false"><span class="_3hrKvq_pill">Safety</span></a></li><li><a href="#mythos" class="_3hrKvq_link" draggable="false"><span class="_3hrKvq_pill">Claude Mythos 5.1</span></a></li><li><a href="#cost-and-availability" class="_3hrKvq_link" draggable="false"><span class="_3hrKvq_pill">Cost and availability</span></a></li></ol></nav><header class="RCsuha_launch-hero RCsuha_scene" data-fx-ready=""><img src="https://origin-ten-jet.vercel.app/sites/anthropic/pages-assets/97518b0f8c93ca1e15e2.png" class="RCsuha_canvas RCsuha_drawn" aria-hidden="true" data-engine="three.js r182" style="width: 375px; height: 844px;"><div class="RCsuha_words"><p class="text-prehead-tracked RCsuha_date" style="animation: auto ease 0s 1 normal none running none; filter: none; opacity: 1;">September 2026</p><h1 class="RCsuha_title" aria-label="Claude Fable 5.1 and Mythos 5.1"><span class="RCsuha_title-line" aria-hidden="true" style="animation: auto ease 0s 1 normal none running none; filter: none; opacity: 1;"><span class="RCsuha_title-claude">:Claude:</span> Fable<!-- --> 5.1</span><span class="RCsuha_title-line RCsuha_title-line-second" aria-hidden="true" style="animation: auto ease 0s 1 normal none running none; filter: none; opacity: 1;">and Mythos 5.1</span></h1><nav class="RCsuha_index" aria-label="Contents"><a href="#introduction" class="RCsuha_index-row" style="--fx-row:0"><span class="RCsuha_index-row-inner" style="animation: auto ease 0s 1 normal none running none; filter: none; opacity: 1;"><span class="RCsuha_index-number">[<!-- -->1<!-- -->]</span><span class="RCsuha_index-leader" aria-hidden="true">................................................................................................................................................................</span><span>Introduction</span></span></a><a href="#frontier" class="RCsuha_index-row" style="--fx-row:1"><span class="RCsuha_index-row-inner" style="animation: auto ease 0s 1 normal none running none; filter: none; opacity: 1;"><span class="RCsuha_index-number">[<!-- -->2<!-- -->]</span><span class="RCsuha_index-leader" aria-hidden="true">................................................................................................................................................................</span><span>A new performance frontier</span></span></a><a href="#scientific-research" class="RCsuha_index-row" style="--fx-row:2"><span class="RCsuha_index-row-inner" style="animation: auto ease 0s 1 normal none running none; filter: none; opacity: 1;"><span class="RCsuha_index-number">[<!-- -->3<!-- -->]</span><span class="RCsuha_index-leader" aria-hidden="true">................................................................................................................................................................</span><span>Scientific research</span></span></a><a href="#safety-security-and-alignment" class="RCsuha_index-row" style="--fx-row:3"><span class="RCsuha_index-row-inner" style="animation: auto ease 0s 1 normal none running none; filter: none; opacity: 1;"><span class="RCsuha_index-number">[<!-- -->4<!-- -->]</span><span class="RCsuha_index-leader" aria-hidden="true">................................................................................................................................................................</span><span>Safety, security, and alignment</span></span></a><a href="#mythos" class="RCsuha_index-row" style="--fx-row:4"><span class="RCsuha_index-row-inner" style="animation: auto ease 0s 1 normal none running none; filter: none; opacity: 1;"><span class="RCsuha_index-number">[<!-- -->5<!-- -->]</span><span class="RCsuha_index-leader" aria-hidden="true">................................................................................................................................................................</span><span>Claude Mythos 5.1</span></span></a></nav></div><span class="RCsuha_credit">Made with Fable 5.1</span><div class="RCsuha_looks" role="group" aria-label="Time of day"><button type="button" class="RCsuha_look RCsuha_on" title="Noon" aria-label="Noon" style="--sw: #7ea9de;">Noon</button><button type="button" class="RCsuha_look" title="Night" aria-label="Night" style="--sw: #1a2237;">Night</button><button type="button" class="RCsuha_look" title="Morning" aria-label="Morning" style="--sw: #dcc4b3;">Morning</button></div></header><article class="_6Ouj0G_article"><div class="PA4F1q_body JsRY2q_launch-body" data-article-body="true" data-theme="ivory"><span id="introduction" class="JsRY2q_anchor"></span><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column PA4F1q_article-summary">We’re introducing Claude Fable 5.1 and Claude Mythos 5.1. They’re the world’s most advanced models for coding and knowledge work—and their research capabilities offer an early glimpse of how AI models will contribute to scientific progress.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Fable 5.1 and Claude Mythos 5.1 are the same model, but with different levels of safeguards. Fable 5.1 is generally available, while Mythos 5.1 is available only through our trusted access programs; its safeguards are specifically designed to support work in cybersecurity and the life sciences.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Alongside its increased capabilities, Fable 5.1 takes important steps towards addressing the feedback we’ve received from customers on price, data retention, and safeguards.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Price<em>.</em></strong> Fable 5.1 will cost an estimated 25% less than Fable 5 for typical workloads, wherever usage is billed by token. This is because we’re reducing our pricing on cache reads (where the model reads inputs that have already been processed and stored). For highly agentic work, the savings will often be much larger—up to approximately 45%.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Data retention<em>. </em></strong>Our new system of Enterprise Frontier Safeguards (EFS) gives customers complete privacy (the same as a zero data retention policy) while still being state-of-the-art at preventing adversarial use. EFS works by storing data in cloud infrastructure controlled entirely by the customer, not Anthropic. It will be made available to enterprise customers in phases, beginning later this fall. Until EFS is available, eligible customers will be able to use Fable 5.1 with zero data retention.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Safeguards<em>. </em></strong>We’ve improved our safeguards to reduce false positives (where the system flags benign content). In cybersecurity, our newest safeguards block 60% fewer false positives than before. In part, this is because Fable 5.1 can now be used to discover software vulnerabilities—though not to develop exploits for them. In biology, we’ve established an access program, developed in partnership with the US government, to enable access to Claude Mythos 5.1’s advanced biology capabilities. We expect to open enrollment for scientists soon.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="frontier">A new performance frontier</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Fable 5.1 sets a new standard for coding, knowledge work, and long-running problem-solving tasks. The charts below show that Fable 5.1 is capable of much higher performance than its predecessor, Fable 5. And when set to Low or Medium effort, Fable 5.1 achieves results similar to or better than Fable 5’s at a much lower cost. (Note that Fable 5.1 defaults to High effort in Claude Code, and to Medium in Claude Cowork and on Claude.ai.)</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="viewSwitcher"><section class="zKqBAG_scroll" aria-hidden="true"><div class="zKqBAG_spacer" style="width: 420px;"></div></div></div><figcaption class="zKqBAG_caption JQQKta_rich"><p>Fable 5.1 was evaluated with its production safeguards enabled. On tasks where these safeguards intervened, Fable 5.1 and Fable 5 scored a zero on OSWorld 2.0, and Fable 5 scored a zero on AutomationBench. In all other interventions from our safeguards, cybersecurity tasks were completed by Claude Opus 4.8, and biology tasks were completed by Claude Opus 5. This likely reduces the performance of Fable 5.1 and Fable 5 on these benchmarks.</p></figcaption></figure></div><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Our early-access partners noticed these performance upgrades, and also picked up on more qualitative improvements in the model’s outputs. aria-labelledby="_R_g7inpfkq6btb_-tab-0"><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“In internal benchmarks, Claude Fable 5.1 solves more of our coding problems than Fable 5 or Opus 5, and achieves state of the art on trading intuition. While prior models became hard to follow the longer they worked, Fable 5.1 remains readable over long, multi-step tasks.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Jane Street Capital</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Craig Falls, Head of Quantitative Research</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_g7inpfkq6btb_-panel-1" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_g7inpfkq6btb_-tab-1" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“We’re moving our Opus 5 traffic in Devin to Claude Fable 5.1 on launch day. It matched or edged out Fable 5 in our testing at a lower cost per task, and with the new cache read pricing a Fable-class model is finally economical for the workloads we’d kept on Opus, starting with code review.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Cognition</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Walden Yan, Co-founder and CPO</span></div></div></article></div><button type="button" class="_1oJwFW_reveal" tabindex="-1" aria-hidden="true"></button></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_g7inpfkq6btb_-panel-2" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_g7inpfkq6btb_-tab-2" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“A particular piece of code had an extremely rare crash, about one in a million runs, that nobody on our team had explained in four to five years. Every model I tried, including Fable 5, missed it. Claude Fable 5.1 was the first to find it. It disassembled an external vendor library, matched it against the core dump, and traced the crash to a bug in that library. The time it would have taken to conduct that analysis is hard to justify.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Millennium</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Damien, Senior Portfolio Manager</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_g7inpfkq6btb_-panel-3" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_g7inpfkq6btb_-tab-3" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“Claude Fable 5.1 built a complex prototype in about three days. It did initial research across all of our services code and documentation to produce a novel and extensible design. It then ran for hours unattended, with strong verification loops, to implement the entire prototype. I would wake up in the morning to the next phase finished, with a full visual walkthrough of what it built and clear evidence of its success.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">MongoDB</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Ron Sanzone, Staff Software Engineer</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_slide" data-slide="far-next"><div class="_1oJwFW_panel" id="_R_g7inpfkq6btb_-panel-4" role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby="_R_g7inpfkq6btb_-tab-4" aria-hidden="true" inert=""><article class="_16pwma_card _1oJwFW_card"><div class="_16pwma_body"><span class="_16pwma_label">Quote</span><blockquote class="_16pwma_quote"><p class="body-2 serif">“It’s friendly Fable. The gain comes from its ability to dig harder into grounded, authoritative sources: on one earnings question, it went straight to the call transcript and captured the exact figures management cited, where other models leaned on secondary coverage.”</p></blockquote></div><div class="_16pwma_split"><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Company</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Samaya</span></div><div class="_16pwma_cell"><span class="_16pwma_label">Author</span><span class="_16pwma_value">Yuhao Zhang, Research Lead</span></div></div></article></div></div><div class="_1oJwFW_veil _1oJwFW_veil-left" aria-hidden="true"></div><div class="_1oJwFW_veil _1oJwFW_veil-right" aria-hidden="true"></div></div></section></div><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="scientific-research">Scientific research</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">We tested the scientific research capabilities of Claude Fable 5.1 and Claude Mythos 5.1 across a wide range of domains. What we found—which includes the early examples we share below—adds to the evidence that AI models will soon make important contributions to scientific discovery.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Molecular design<em>.</em> </strong>Many modern medicines work by binding to targets within the body to block, activate, or deliver something to them. High-affinity binders are necessary for drugs to work at lower doses; designing one is the first step in the development process for many common drug modalities. To see how well Claude Mythos 5.1 could do at this task, we gave the model access to open-source protein design and folding tools and sent its designs to two external organizations for experimental validation. Mythos 5.1 proved able to design very high-affinity binders. On three targets, <sup class="caption PA4F1q_sup">[3]</sup> its binding affinities were 10 times higher than the best designs submitted to <a id="base-ui-_R_7qk7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://proteinbase.com/competitions" rel="noopener noreferrer">Adaptyv Bio’s protein design competitions</a>. Its hit rate (that is, the number of designs that were viable binders) was the strongest we’ve measured to date: it reached nearly 50% across 12 targets. (Hit rates of 10–15% are typical in protein design today.)</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="videoSwitcher"><figure class="xFM4Hq_video-switcher"><div class="xFM4Hq_frame"><video class="xFM4Hq_poster" src="https://origin-ten-jet.vercel.app/sites/anthropic/pages-assets/7b7206e6f99097ec105a.webm" poster="https://origin-ten-jet.vercel.app/sites/anthropic/pages-assets/7086876e41affdb69862.jpg" loop="" muted="" playsinline=""></video><button type="button" class="xFM4Hq_play" aria-label="Play: Claude-designed protein binders (orange) for each of 12 targets (grey). Every design in the video was confirmed to bind in the lab. Structures shown are ESMFold2 predictions."><svg viewBox="0 0 24 24" width="24" height="24" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M7 4 L20 12 L7 20 Z"></path></svg></button></div><figcaption class="xFM4Hq_caption">Claude-designed protein binders (orange) for each of 12 targets (grey). Every design in the video was confirmed to bind in the lab. Structures shown are ESMFold2 predictions.</figcaption></figure></div><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Computational analysis and modeling</strong>. Claude Fable 5.1 trained a neural network to create a new, high-resolution elevation map of a third of the planet Venus. Its work was based on radar images taken by NASA’s Magellan mission more than 30 years ago and a <a id="base-ui-_R_7mm7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://agupubs.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1029/2012EO120002" rel="noopener noreferrer">map</a> that already existed for one-fifth of the planet. Claude’s new map now reveals details down to two to three kilometers, rather than 10 to 20, and shows heights up to 25% more accurately than before.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">We’re <a id="base-ui-_R_3sn7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://zenodo.org/records/22164484" rel="noopener noreferrer">releasing this map</a> under a Creative Commons license in advance of upcoming NASA VERITAS and ESA EnVision missions, in hopes that it might help them determine which geologic features to target for future observation.</p><div class="n4MhcW_carousel-count">04</div></div><button aria-label="Next media" data-component="Button" data-variant="tertiary" class="f9ZZrG_button f9ZZrG_corner-small f9ZZrG_variant-tertiary f9ZZrG_size-small f9ZZrG_icon-format-icon-only"><span class="f9ZZrG_icon"><svg width="20" height="20" viewBox="0 0 20 20" fill="currentColor" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" aria-hidden="true" style="flex-shrink: 0;"><path d="M11.1465 4.64648C11.3417 4.45127 11.6582 4.45136 11.8535 4.64648L16.8535 9.64649L16.916 9.72267C16.9703 9.80418 17 9.90061 17 10C17 10.1326 16.9473 10.2598 16.8535 9.50001H15.293L11.1465 5.35352C10.9514 5.15826 10.9513 4.8417 11.1465 4.64648Z"></path></svg></span></button></div></div></div><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Computational biology<em>.</em></strong> In computational biology, it’s common to run task-specific machine learning models on GPUs. The speed of these models is therefore a bottleneck to research progress. Mythos 5.1 provided one solution to this problem: by writing custom GPU kernels and caching their intermediate results, it sped up seven open-source deep learning models by up to 2.5 times (with identical outputs).</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">The benefits of such speed-ups accumulate quickly. In any given experiment, biologists might run these models thousands of times (for example, testing every possible mutation near every human gene). On analyses like these, the optimized models cut estimated GPU costs by 30–60%. This kind of optimization would normally take a team of performance engineers weeks, and is often unaffordable for academic labs. Mythos 5.1 was able to do it in just days, using the publicly available source code alone. We plan to open-source these optimizations soon.</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="viewSwitcher"><section class="kN30kq_root"><div class="kN30kq_tabs" role="tablist" Evo 2 40B saves more on a whole job (2.3x) than per forward (1.4x) because some of its optimizations only pay off across many sequences.</p></div></figure></div></div></div></section></div><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As our models’ scientific capabilities improve, our investment in scientific progress is also growing. Last week, we previewed the <a target="_blank" href="/news/model-hardware-standard-research-preview" rel="noopener noreferrer">Model Hardware Standard</a>, which allows Claude to directly and safely operate laboratory equipment. We’ve also recently <a target="_blank" href="/news/expanding-support-for-scientists" rel="noopener noreferrer">expanded our support for scientists</a> through our <a target="_blank" href="/news/ai-for-science-program" rel="noopener noreferrer">AI for Science program</a>, which provides free credits to researchers working on high-impact scientific projects, and we are offering steeply discounted usage through our new <a id="base-ui-_R_fgs7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://claude.com/programs/team-plan-for-scientists" rel="noopener noreferrer">Claude Team plan for scientists</a>.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="safety-security-and-alignment">Safety, security, and alignment</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">AI models’ agentic capabilities have become much more powerful over the past two years. But as we’ve <a target="_blank" href="/news/investigating-incidents-cybersecurity-evals" rel="noopener noreferrer">documented</a>, greater autonomy comes with new risks. Work on safety, security, and alignment needs to advance at the same pace as AI capabilities. Yesterday, we <a target="_blank" href="/news/improving-alignment-security-efforts" rel="noopener noreferrer">published a report</a> describing how we are improving our own alignment and security efforts</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Prior to releasing Claude Fable 5.1 and Claude Mythos 5.1, we (and, in some cases, external researchers) subjected the models to extensive testing for risks across many areas. We describe these efforts in full in our <a target="_blank" href="/claude-fable-5-1-mythos-5-1-system-card" rel="noopener noreferrer">System Card</a>; below is a brief summary.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Chemical and biological risks</strong>. We tested the extent to which Claude Mythos 5.1 could help create chemical or biological weapons. This involved expert red-teaming, automated evaluations, and a tabletop exercise that paired PhD-level biologists with AI experts, testing whether the models could match human specialists’ performance. Mythos 5.1’s capabilities are greater than those of Mythos 5. However, our evaluations indicate that it still falls short of the next risk tier defined in our <a target="_blank" href="/responsible-scaling-policy" rel="noopener noreferrer">Responsible Scaling Policy</a>. We are therefore deploying Mythos 5.1 with the <a target="_blank" href="/news/improving-fable-5-s-biology-safeguards" rel="noopener noreferrer">same safeguards</a> that we applied to Mythos 5, which restrict access to research biology capabilities.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Cyber risks</strong>. We ran a suite of evaluations to assess the cyber capabilities of Claude Mythos 5.1 (with cybersecurity safeguards off). Overall, the model demonstrates the strongest cyber capabilities of any model we’ve released, though it still falls within the lower category of risk in our <a target="_blank" href="/news/compliance-framework-SB53" rel="noopener noreferrer">Frontier Compliance Framework</a>. We also performed extensive stress-testing of our cybersecurity safeguards for Fable 5.1: in addition to our own dynamic evaluation of their robustness, we commissioned external testing from two organizations, along with automated testing by <a id="base-ui-_R_fb27inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://www.grayswan.ai/" rel="noopener noreferrer">Gray Swan</a>. As with Fable 5 and Opus 5, we have not found evidence of a <a target="_blank" href="/news/fable-safeguards-jailbreak-framework" rel="noopener noreferrer">critical-severity jailbreak</a> for these safeguards.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Agentic safety</strong>. We ran evaluations of how Claude Mythos 5.1 responds to malicious requests and prompt injections (adversarial instructions hidden within content processed by AI models). It refused malicious agentic coding and computer use requests at a comparable rate to Mythos 5, Sonnet 5, and Opus 5, and it is our most robust model to date on an external <a target="_blank" href="/claude-fable-5-1-mythos-5-1-system-card" rel="noopener noreferrer">prompt injection benchmark</a>.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Alignment<em>.</em></strong> We tested the model’s behavior through static and interactive behavioral evaluations, analyses of its internal thinking using <a target="_blank" href="/research/natural-language-autoencoders" rel="noopener noreferrer">natural language autoencoders</a>, misalignment-related capability evaluations, a review of our training data, and analyses of our internal pilot use. We also received reports from external testing.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Our automated behavioral audit found that Claude Mythos 5.1 is better aligned across most metrics than its predecessor, Mythos 5. The model is significantly less likely than Mythos 5 to try to access resources outside of its test environment when assigned an otherwise impossible task. It is also less likely than Mythos 5 to use motivated reasoning to justify its actions (for instance, by reasoning that the situation is a simulation or evaluation), and it is less likely to ignore explicit constraints in pursuit of users’ goals. From our review of its training data, Mythos 5.1 both attempts reward hacking (or cheating), and succeeds at it, at a lower overall rate than Mythos 5.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Though generally our alignment evaluations showed improvements, our testing found the model can still sometimes bypass approvals and auto-mode classifiers (as we discuss in more detail in our <a target="_blank" href="/claude-fable-5-1-mythos-5-1-system-card" rel="noopener noreferrer">System Card</a>). There are also limitations to the coverage provided by our alignment assessment. Currently, our automated behavioral audit provides less visibility into very long-context work and multi-agent settings. We also have less coverage of impossible tasks (which can elicit more abnormal and misaligned behavior) than we’d like, although we’ve recently made improvements in this domain and are working hard to continue doing so.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">We have also improved our safeguards so that they allow our models to be more useful without compromising on safety. We describe these changes below.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Automated safeguards for enterprises<em>.</em></strong> <a target="_blank" href="/news/enterprise-frontier-safeguards" rel="noopener noreferrer">Enterprise Frontier Safeguards</a> (EFS) allows us to detect and respond to misuse of our models while still providing our enterprise customers the privacy of a zero data retention agreement. With EFS, customers store their data on their own cloud infrastructure, rather than on Anthropic’s systems; any human review is, by default, done by the customer themselves, rather than Anthropic. We developed EFS in close collaboration with more than 100 customers across industries like financial services, healthcare, manufacturing, telecom, law, retail, and the public sector, and with our cloud partners at Amazon Web Services, Google Cloud, and Microsoft Azure.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">EFS will be supported on Claude Code, Claude Enterprise, the Claude Platform, Amazon Bedrock, Claude Platform on AWS, Google’s Agent Platform, and Microsoft Foundry. It’s rolling out in phases, starting this fall. As noted above, customers who are eligible for EFS can use Fable 5.1 (and Fable 5) with zero data retention until EFS is ready. You can read more about EFS <a target="_blank" href="/news/enterprise-frontier-safeguards" rel="noopener noreferrer">here</a>; to request access, please complete <a id="base-ui-_R_7p97inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" href="https://claude.com/form/enterprise-frontier-safeguards">this form</a>.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>More precise safeguards for biology and cybersecurity<em>. </em></strong>In the past few months, we’ve made progress in making our safeguards for Fable 5.1 more precise: ensuring that they’re less likely to flag benign content (like queries about medical issues or cyberdefenders using the model to make their systems safer), but still ensuring they provide robust protection against genuine threats.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As we <a target="_blank" href="/news/improving-fable-5-s-biology-safeguards" rel="noopener noreferrer">recently shared</a>, our latest biology safeguards for Fable 5.1 and Fable 5 fire 85% less often for benign requests related to elementary biology and medical questions (relative to those that launched with Fable 5). However, queries related to research and development in the life sciences will still be directed to our Opus models. We’re making the model’s life sciences capabilities available to professionals through an access program for Claude Mythos 5.1 that we’ve developed in partnership with the US government, which we discuss below.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">With Fable 5.1, we’re updating our cybersecurity safeguards to be more precise. We’re also now allowing Fable 5.1 to be used for identifying software vulnerabilities—that is, to conduct the kind of defensive work that improves software security. As a result of these changes, Claude Code users can expect an average of around 60% fewer interventions per session from our cyber safeguards, relative to the previous safeguards on Fable 5. Our safeguards do, however, still redirect several kinds of dual-use cybersecurity tasks (tasks that might have helpful <em>or</em> harmful applications) to our Opus models. This includes penetration testing, exploit generation, and binary-based vulnerability scanning.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text"><strong>Anti-distillation mechanisms<em>.</em></strong> Distillation is a method used to extract the capabilities of advanced models. It is often employed on an industrial scale, using thousands of fake accounts. Distillation is a safety risk, since the distilled capabilities can subsequently be released without adequate safeguards. Fable 5.1 comes with strengthened mechanisms to make distillation attacks harder. For example, it is no longer possible for new API accounts (those created from today onwards) to manually edit Claude’s prior context in a multi-turn conversation while preserving the transcript of Claude’s prior thinking. This closes off a common, publicly documented distillation technique, which allowed distillers to illicitly extract Claude’s thinking. We’re rolling out the change gradually, to minimize disruption: existing accounts are not currently affected by this change, though it will apply to all users with future model releases. A small number of customers’ custom integrations will then be affected. Our <a id="base-ui-_R_7nd7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/16761192" rel="noopener noreferrer">Help Center article</a> explains more about this change and the adjustments that developers can make.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="mythos">Trusted access for Claude Mythos 5.1</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Mythos 5.1 is identical to Fable 5.1, but it offers more permissive safeguards for vetted individuals and organizations whose work is affected by the cybersecurity and life sciences restrictions outlined above. It will be available through two trusted access programs:</p><ul class="PA4F1q_reading-column text-body-1-serif post-text"><li><strong>Cyber Verification Program:</strong> The CVP currently provides access to certain Opus- and Sonnet-class models with reduced cyber safeguards for defensive security work. In the near future, this program will also include access to Claude Mythos-class models. <a id="base-ui-_R_urh7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://portal.anthropic.com/programs/cvp" rel="noopener noreferrer">Apply to join the CVP here</a>.</li><li><strong>Life Sciences Verification Program:</strong> The LSVP is designed so that life sciences professionals can use Claude Mythos 5.1 with safeguards designed for professional research and development activities (while all other safeguards remain in place). In partnership with the US government, we have enrolled our first participants, and we plan to expand access to this program to the broader life sciences community.</li></ul><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">In addition to these trusted access programs, <a id="base-ui-_R_3ti7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://claude.com/product/claude-security" rel="noopener noreferrer">Claude Security</a>, our product that scans codebases for vulnerabilities and suggests patches for human review, is now also powered by Claude Mythos 5.1.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="compliance-with-the-eu-ai-act">Compliance with the EU AI Act</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">In July 2026, Anthropic (along with <a id="base-ui-_R_3tk7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://digital-strategy.ec.europa.eu/en/news/strong-backing-code-practice-transparency-ai-generated-content" rel="noopener noreferrer">190 other signatories</a>, including several other major AI model providers) signed the EU AI Act’s Code of Practice on Transparency of AI-Generated Content.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">This required us to add a watermark—a numerical way of determining the likelihood that Claude was involved in writing a piece of text—to the outputs of models released after August 2, 2026. As we <a target="_blank" href="/news/claude-text-watermark" rel="noopener noreferrer">recently explained</a>, this watermark is invisible to anyone who does not have the detection API. It has no practical impact on the quality or content of Claude’s outputs and contains no information about the user, their organization, or their conversations with Claude.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">The Act also required us to provide a way for users to tell whether a text likely contains the watermark. We are thus rolling out a detection API in private preview. It is currently available to eligible organizations (such as regulators, law enforcement, media, fact-checkers, independent researchers, educational organizations, and EU civil society groups) as required under EU law. It is also available for enterprises that are similarly obligated to verify watermarking for their own compliance with the Act. We plan to expand access to the detection API over time. You can register interest in access <a id="base-ui-_R_3tm7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://forms.gle/9tGA33hPJJwtHsMk9" rel="noopener noreferrer">here</a>.</p><h2 class="tOQyXa_headline-4--serif PA4F1q_reading-column post-heading" data-component="Headline" id="cost-and-availability">Cost and availability</h2><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">Claude Fable 5.1 is available today on all platforms, including Amazon Web Services, Google Cloud, and Microsoft Azure. Developers can get started with <code class="nsPAba_code">claude-fable-5-1</code> on the Claude API.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As mentioned above, we have reduced the price of Fable 5.1’s cache reads (where the model reuses context it has already processed) wherever usage is billed by token, such as on our API. Cache reads now cost 75% less, or $0.25 per million tokens.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">This change leads to a substantial reduction in the overall cost of running the model. For typical workloads, costs are reduced by around 25% relative to Fable 5. For complex coding and highly agentic tasks, the savings could be up to around 45%. The graph below illustrates why this change makes such a big difference:</p><div class="PA4F1q_media-column" data-type="chart"><figure class="_3ia3wq_frame"><div class="_3ia3wq_panel"><figcaption In parallel, we’re continuing our work to bring many of the improvements of Fable 5.1 to the rest of our model family.</p><p class="PA4F1q_reading-column post-text">As discussed above, Claude Mythos 5.1 is available to vetted cyberdefenders and life scientists. Currently, it is only available to a set of US organizations, though we’re coordinating with the US government to expand access to a broader set of domestic and international partners as quickly as possible. To register interest in access to Claude Mythos 5.1 for cyberdefense through the CVP, <a id="base-ui-_R_3tu7inpfkq6btb_" data-base-ui-tooltip-trigger="" target="_blank" href="https://portal.anthropic.com/programs/cvp" rel="noopener noreferrer">head here</a>.</p></div><section id="get-started" class="qvEXQG_get-started _6Ouj0G_reading-column"><h2 class="text-body-1-serif qvEXQG_heading">Get started</h2><div class="qvEXQG_actions"><a href="https://claude.ai/" class="f9ZZrG_button f9ZZrG_corner-small f9ZZrG_variant-primary f9ZZrG_size-medium" rel="noopener" target="_blank">Try Claude</a><a href="https://platform.claude.com/" class="f9ZZrG_button f9ZZrG_corner-small f9ZZrG_variant-secondary f9ZZrG_size-medium" rel="noopener" target="_blank">Start building</a></div></section><section id="footnotes" class="_FS4_a_footnotes _6Ouj0G_reading-column"><h2 class="text-body-1-serif _FS4_a_heading">Footnotes</h2><div class="_FS4_a_columns"><p class="_FS4_a_note"><sup class="_FS4_a_label">1</sup> <strong>Terminal-Bench-Science 0.1:</strong> The standard error is ±3.5–4.5 pts per model. The public leaderboard (3 trials/task, Claude Code harness) reports Claude Opus 5 at 30.0% and Claude Fable 5 at 21.4%; our setup reproduces them at 29.0% and 24.7%, respectively, both within noise.</p><p class="_FS4_a_note"><sup class="_FS4_a_label">2</sup> <strong>OSWorld 2.0:</strong> Scores are on the benchmark authors’ August 2026 task release; Fable 5 and Opus 5 were re-run under the same conditions. Because the task files differ from earlier releases, these numbers aren't directly comparable to previously published OSWorld 2.0 results, which is why no competitor score is shown</p><p class="_FS4_a_note"><sup class="_FS4_a_label">3</sup> <!-- -->These three targets are (EGFR, Nipah G, 15-PGDH) and come from <a href="https://proteinbase.com/competitions" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Adaptyv Bio’s protein design competitions</a>. The Nipah G comparison is against <em>de novo</em> designs targeting the receptor-binding site on the G head (best: ~8–12 nM, <a href="https://proteinbase.com/proteins/shy-otter-jade" target="_blank" rel="noopener">N1032</a>). A <em>de novo</em> entry from <a href="https://blog.escalante.bio/winning-the-de-novo-portion-of-the-adaptyv-nipah-binder-competition/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Nick Boyd/Escalante Bio</a> that targets a different region (the stalk) reached ~1.4 nM (<a href="https://proteinbase.com/proteins/shy-eagle-fern" target="_blank" rel="noopener">design_7</a>), comparable to our best binder.</p></div></section><section class="Y_7aRW_end-matter _6Ouj0G_reading-column"><div id="further-reading" class="Y_7aRW_column"><h2 class="text-body-1-serif Y_7aRW_heading">Further
